ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members and fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! Starting the 19th meeting of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meetings cannot be attended by the following Members: Erika Dekleva, Janja Sluga until 3 p.m., Anita Koleša until 3 p.m., Iva Dimik from 12.30 p.m., Matjaž Hanžek, Kamal Izidor Shaker, Jan Škoberne until 4 p.m., Marijan Pojbič, Marjan Dolinšek between 11.30 and 13.30, Ivan Škodnik until 12 p.m. and mag. Matthew Tonin. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to four. Agenda item - Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Act on Social Security, Summary Procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the Minister for Labour, the family, social affairs and equal opportunities of Dr Anji Kopač Mrak.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u2	Good morning! Thank you for your word, Madam President. Messengers and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Social Protection Act contains three sets of amendments. The first set of amendments is to provide a legal basis for drawing on European funds for the implementation of a pilot social activation project aimed at the development and placement of social activation in the social protection system and is closely linked in its core to the planned reorganisation of social work centres. The legal basis for pre-testing the organisation of activities or individual social protection services for the implementation of the above mentioned pilot projects is also introduced. The second set of amendments concerns the regulation of the status of individual types of social security programmes, the determination of their duration and the way in which they are co-financed. Here we would like to stress that we are not introducing new obligations for the country or the municipalities by means of a change, but we are simply clarifying the co-financing of programmes already implemented from the budgets of the countries and municipalities. The amendment of the law sets out the scope of the duration of social security programmes, the way in which they are financed, the sources of funding, and a more precise co-financing process through public tendering. However, the third set of amendments introduced by the proposal is a different arrangement of traineeships. By means of a proposal for a law, we are abolishing compulsory social protection traineeship, which has so far been a prerequisite for accession to the application of a professional examination. Following a new proposal for a law, individuals can approach the social security professional examination when they have 6 or 9 months of working experience in the field of social protection. 6 months of work experience is envisaged for all graduates of higher or higher education in the field of social work, since at the Ministry we estimate that the students of the Faculty of Social Work themselves have gained a certain amount and a sufficient amount of work experience in the field of social protection during their studies according to their study programme. The Ministry will also set up a working group in which representatives of the Social Chamber of Slovenia, professions and young people will be able to identify together the work experience of those who are recognised. Let me mention some of the other minor changes that are in this bill. One is the decommissioning of administrative procedures, i.e. the removal of certain administrative barriers relating to individual consents, to deadlines and the like. We are therefore talking about procedures that have so far burdened both indirect budgetary users, namely centres for social work, labour centres and others and the Ministry, while not contributing to better quality of operations and operations. The proposal also introduces the conditions for the award of the status of societies in the public interest in a way that, in addition to societies, other legal persons who do not earn profits, i.e. non-profit legal persons such as institutions, institutions, private institutions, institutions, and private institutions, will be able to obtain a new status in the public interest. This amendment of the law also aligns the Social Protection Act with the Labour Inspection Act. Thank you.

On 5th this year, the proposal for a law on social protection was addressed as the working body of the mother. Following a decision by the College of the President of the National Assembly, the law was examined following a reduced procedure. We have invited the widest interested professional, including the public and representatives of the Government, to the work of the Committee. The Commission of the Council of State for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons has also previously considered the amendment of the law and has written in its report to support all proposed solutions. The Committee itself said that the proposal for a law introduced a legal basis for drawing on European funds for the implementation of a pilot project aimed at the development and placement of social activation services in the social protection system. The proposal also regulates the status of individual types of social protection programmes and provides the appropriate basis for their funding, abolishes traineeships as a mandatory condition for access to a professional examination and shortens the period of implementation. It also removes some unnecessary administrative obstacles. As it is said, a very broad professional and, among other things, an interested public participated in the debate. All the debaters agreed with the amendments tabled, but some felt that they were too large. The Slovenian Student Organisation and the Youth Union, plus the proposal for a law, also point out that, apart from the analysis of traineeships in the Republic of Slovenia last June in the field of traineeships, no further in-depth analysis has been carried out, which is expected in the future. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee also adopted any proposed amendments which relate or respond to the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service, which reviewed the law within its competence and made comments thereon. At the conclusion, we also voted on all the articles of the bill together and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared on the basis of Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. Thank you very much.

The area of social protection requires urgent changes. The law in force is old. It was adopted in 1992. In the last 20 years, however, there has been a major development of the area to which we must follow. The bill also provides a legal basis for drawing on European funds for the implementation of a pilot project aimed at the development and placement of social protection services, which will be pilotly co-financed by the European funds of the new operational programme for the implementation of European cohesion policy. In the case of social activation, it is important to address people in an integrated manner, to deal with people who are exposed to social exclusion and, for various reasons, to the risk of poverty, to face social pressures, to be excluded from the labour market and to be long-term recipients of social assistance. These are different groups of individuals facing various obstacles due to alcoholism, illicit drugs, violence, mental health problems and the like, which make it difficult for them to reintegrate into society. In doing so, it is important to establish an interdisciplinary approach that will be adapted to individual groups in need of assistance and that different existing programmes also be linked. Today, the main part of the implementation of various forms of professional assistance in the field of social protection has been brought to the private sector. In this way, most of this aid is provided by NGOs, which often take the initiative to provide certain services, which are often co-financed by the government on the basis of the public interest identified. The bill therefore regulates the status of individual types of social security programmes and provides an appropriate basis for their financing. In this regard, it should also be noted that the draft law also lays down specific conditions for the award of public interest status in the field of social protection, thus allowing for the granting of this status to other private legal persons who are not established for profit purposes and are not necessarily a society. One of the long-awaited solutions in the draft law is the amended regulation of traineeships in the field of social protection. For traineeships are removed as a mandatory condition for access to a professional examination and reduces the duration of traineeships. This allows candidates who have not carried out traineeships to join the professional examination and have 6 or 9 months of experience in the field of social protection. It also took 6 or 9 months, depending on the working experience of the individual and the type of education, according to the proposal of the law. We will support this proposal of the Modern Centre Party in the Parliamentary Group. Thank you.

The basic starting point of social protection measures is to ensure dignity and equal opportunities and to prevent social exclusion through social protection programmes that are currently thematically divided into ten public networks. These are designed for individual areas or individual target groups. However, social security programmes can be implemented as public-verified social security programmes, development and experimental social security programmes and complementary programmes. The proposed amendment of the Social Protection Act concerns changes and additions necessary for the further development of the social protection system. The changes relating to social security programmes are crucial to allow some programmes for which Slovenia will draw on European funds in the new financial perspective of European cohesion policy by 2020. Amendments to the programmes will apply what is enshrined in the Resolution on the National Social Protection Programme, set out by 2020, which means that the programmes will be more detailed and in line with the visions, resources and means of financing will be determined and the verification and extent of the duration of the programme will be determined. Long-term care and deinstitutionalisation are two key points that are of great importance in an increasingly ageing society. At present, a little less than €500 million is earmarked for long-term care, but it is clear that more than that should be invested in the care of the elderly and others in need of long-term care, since, among other things, we should not neglect the informal assistance provided within families and by denying family members an extremely large contribution to both financial and human aspects. In the Desus Group today, we see the amendments and additions to the Social Protection Act as part of the contribution to strengthening this area in the future at least through social protection programmes. According to the competent ministry, this proposal for a law is intended to establish the legal basis for pilot projects relating to social activation and the experimental provision of services, which should later be part of the Long-Term Care Act. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u14	Dear Mr President, Madam Minister with a team, colleagues and colleagues! In the 20 years since the existing Social Protection Act, he has already undergone a number of amendments and additions. In parallel, circumstances have developed and significantly changed the area of social protection programmes and have affected the development of new programmes as a reflection of emerging needs. Within the private sector, NGOs have been providing various forms of professional assistance for many years, and these organisations are often the generator of the development of new services, often co-financed by the government. The real area of social protection is an area where such effectiveness and efficiency are reflected in a specific form, as this is how very quality and user-friendly programmes are designed. This bill regulates the status of individual types of social protection programmes and provides an appropriate basis for their funding. With the amendment, we are changing the areas of social protection that currently need to be changed most. This is a matter of drawing up European funds in the field of social activation and coordinated community care on the basis of the application of legal bases, allowing for the prior testing of the organisation of activities or individual social security services or programmes. Another important area of change is the normative regulation of the status of financing and the implementation of social protection programmes aimed at preventing and addressing the social pressures of individual vulnerable groups. At the same time, these amendments also seek to remove certain unnecessary administrative obstacles that delay procedures, do not contribute to the higher quality of work, safety or transparency of the procedure, and comply with the applicable provisions on inspection supervision with the provisions of the law governing the inspection and supervision of work and complement the provisions of the applicable law with the provisions on specific conditions which society must comply with in order to obtain the status of society in the field of social protection. One of the most important solutions covered by the proposal for a law, which is necessary in order to develop the profession in the field of the implementation of social protection programmes and in order to expand the possibility of taking a social welfare examination, is the changes in the existing traineeship system in the field of social protection. The proposal removes the condition of compulsory traineeships for the entrance to the professional examination, so that candidates who have not carried out the traineeship may also be admitted to the professional examination but have six or nine months of experience in the field of social protection. The length of the traineeship period depends on the education and work experience gained in the field of social protection. Graduates from a school for social work for six months and all other graduates for nine months of training or work experience in this field. The majority of the debate touched on this particular area, with the need for a clear definition of the concept of work experience largely highlighted. Unfortunately, however, we cannot help but realize that the essence of the problem of traineeships is indeed the problem of providing funding for them. I believe that there is broad consensus and support on the inadmissibility of volunteer traineeships to prohibit such type of traineeship because it is a matter of ordinary exploitation. However, since training is necessary in some areas, it is therefore necessary to provide funding for these traineeships. At this point, too, I would expect us to reach broad consensus. Of course, there will always be funding problems and the related provision of a sufficiently large number of traineeships, but as long as the search for solutions is our common commitment, we will be able to overcome these problems more easily. Since traineeship means activation and entry into the world of work, complete termination is by no means the right solution, as long as we do not find other levers for the employment of young graduates. The Social Democrats will support the bill. Thank you very much.

Few people know that the social security system is even more privatized than health care. I do not believe that any Minister of Health in the draft law would have hoped to write down what Mrs Anja Kopač Mrak wrote, quote: "For more than a decade now, the implementation of the extension of various forms of professional assistance in the field of social protection to the private sector has been taking place in Slovenia." End of quote. The reason for privatisation is that private operators are more friendly and more responsive to the needs of local environments. This is a logic that is essentially a capitulation of public interest to the private and, in fact, neglect of the Ministry of Labour's tasks. If you find that public institutions are unfriendly to users, you should improve the functioning of public institutions, Madam Minister. If they respond too slowly to the needs of the local environment, users and the local environment should play a greater role in decision-making on the functioning of public institutions, Madam Secretary of State. In no way can this be a reason to privatise social protection. No insurmountable obstacle prevents social workers in the public sector from doing social work outside institutions and from adapting more to user requirements. There is another reason for the Ministry's direction. Private operators are cheaper because they employ cheaper labour. When the Ministry issues an invitation to tender for a personal assistant, it shall determine funds which are often insufficient to pay the minimum wage. Tenders are limited in time and personal assistants are employed for a certain period of time. Tenders shall be submitted by unrelated companies that the workers are not in the unions. In short, the Ministry of Labour and Equal Opportunities, while others, creates the chastity of social workers. The same process of social worker grading continues in the field of traineeships. The system solution would be extremely simple, the text of compulsory traineeship being deleted from the traineeship law and replaced by words of work experience. In good social protection systems, knowledge is built up and tested throughout the entire career. Last year, the Ministry promised to replace compulsory traineeships by introducing work with mentors, but the law that is now being amended left only undeclared work experience. If I do some caricatures, someone who hasn't done anything other than serve coffee to the director of the private office will be able to pass the professional exam. Let's leave a joke aside. You have expanded the range of private service providers with public funds. As a condition for self-employment, you have introduced undefined work experience, thus providing the private with a pool of labour that will be forced to work for lower wages than in the public sector or even for free, only to gain work experience. This is, of course, unacceptable and such practices in the United Left will never be supported. Thank you.

Unfortunately, there was still a lot of dissatisfaction on the part of the guests, although I hope to say that the abolition of compulsory traineeships meant, however, easier employment for young people in the area of social protection. From now on, candidates who have not completed their traineeships, but have six or nine months of experience in the field of social protection, will be able to take part in the professional examination, which is a condition for self-employed professional work. Furthermore, we do not deviate significantly from the regulation of other European Union countries by making such a change. We know that in recent years the problems in the field of social protection traineeships have been solved through European funds that paid for the trainee's employment. With this amendment of the law, we will come to an arrangement comparable to other European countries and based on government material, it has also been supported by the professional public. Regardless of the fear that the proposed amendment of the law will close the door to the graduates of the Faculty of Social Work, I think it does not matter because it is essentially a professional examination that allows for the self-employed to perform professional work, and this will only make it easier for them to get a professional examination and thus meet the conditions of employment earlier. Once again, let me stress that the law does not regulate the issue of traineeship in a conceptual sense; only the condition for access to a professional examination is eliminated. However, the amendment proposed by the coalition proposes that the Social Chamber of Slovenia, by means of a general act in addition to the content and method of conducting the professional examination, also lays down the conditions for the performance of the professional examination or to define in detail the concept of working experience in the field of social protection, so that the content of the article is fully preserved and that the work of the person who applies the law will be substantially facilitated. I will support both the amendment and the law. Thank you.

Agenda item — Defence proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on the Republic of Slovenia's measures to protect the stability of banks, a reduced procedure. The draft law was submitted to the assembly by a group of four Members with the first-signed Dr Bojan Dobovšek. The bill is being proposed at the 23rd session of the 4th session. 5. 2016 treated the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent labour force. Since the committee of articles of the draft law was not adopted after the discussion, the second consideration of the bill was completed at the working body meeting. I give the floor to the representatives of the parliamentary groups to present their views. The word is given by the Group of the Party of the Modern Center, for her mag. Dušan Verbich.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u34	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Messengers and Members, nice greetings! The Parliamentary Group of Non-Related Members has tabled a proposal to the National Assembly to delete Article 29, paragraph 7, of the Act on measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to strengthen the stability of banks, which provides that, irrespective of the provisions of the law governing banking governing the assessment of the suitability of members of the management body, it shall carry out in a bank which has received measures to strengthen the stability of banks under this Act and in which the Republic of Slovenia is the sole shareholder, assessing the adequacy of the supervisory board member prior to his appointment in the Slovenian State Holding. When making an assessment of the eligibility of the candidate for a member of the Supervisory Board, the Slovenian State Holding shall take into account the conditions and criteria laid down for a member of the supervisory board of the Bank by the law governing banking and the acts issued on the basis of its issuances. The draft deletion proposed that the Bank of Slovenia, as regulator of the Slovenian banking system and a member of the European Central Bank, should not have any role in the appointment of the members of the Supervisory Board of the largest Slovenian bank, although this role should be clearly laid down in the Banking Act. This claim is certainly not true because the role of the Bank of Slovenia and the European Central Bank is clearly set out in the Banking Act. The Bank of Slovenia shall, under the Banking Act, make a subsequent assessment of the suitability of a member of the Supervisory Board. Thus, Banka Slovenije may, in accordance with Article 57 ZBan-2 to verify that a member of the Supervisory Board fulfils the conditions and duties referred to in Article 55 of the Banking Act 2, decide to conduct an oral interview inviting a member of the supervisory board of the Bank to present in more detail the circumstances which, according to the Bank of Slovenia, are relevant for the assessment of the fulfilment of the conditions and duties of the member of the supervisory board of the Bank itself. In accordance with Article 273 ZBan-2, the Bank of Slovenia is also competent to prohibit the performance of a member of the Supervisory Board in certain cases. With amendments to the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Act on Measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to Strengthen the Stability of Banks, which was in force in December 2015, the Government wanted, by Article 29, paragraph 7, of the same Act, to make preliminary assessments of the candidate as a member of the Supervisory Board and not a bank. This solution is seen from the point of view of the right of the State as the sole shareholder, the owner of the bank, to also perform the function of a preliminary assessment of the eligibility of the candidate, because SDH also exercises and exercises the right of the shareholder on behalf of the Republic of Slovenia at the assembly itself, so that SDH also appoints supervisors in any case. The proposed proposal only follows that it applies to banks which have received State aid and in which the Republic of Slovenia is the sole shareholder and is responsible for the consistent fulfilment of the commitments made to the European Commission under the State aid assessment procedure. One of the commitments also relates to the consolidation of corporate governance in the bank and, of course, a key commitment is the timely completion of the privatisation of state-owned banks. However, additional criteria and conditions for candidates for members of supervisory boards are governed by the Companies Act, as already mentioned in Article 53 of the Banking Act, as regards the performance of the function of a member of the supervisory board of the bank, acts issued on this basis, as well as the EBA guidelines. It follows from the above that SDH must take into account at least the same criteria and conditions that the bank takes into account in the pre-assessment process. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia therefore considers that the seventh paragraph of Article 29 of the Law on the Regulation of Banks’ Credit Stability is without prejudice to the quality of the preliminary verification of candidates. In view of the solutions taken from SDH in order to assess the suitability of the candidate for a member of the Supervisory Board, we do not support the Modern Centre Party Group.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u53	The text is given by the United Left Group, for which Luka Moon.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u54	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Have a nice hello again! In our view, the proposal of unrelated Members makes sense and solves the real problem. Given that the Bank of Slovenia has the responsibility and duty to assess subsequently the suitability of the members of the supervisory boards of banks, it would make sense to give an opinion on eligibility before the appointment itself. This would avoid the cost of repeating the calls and the complications we are seeing now. In short, a predictability that is not in the current regime would be introduced into the procedure. We are convinced, however, that the whole system imposed by the European Directive should be repaired and that it actually legitimizes the conflict of interest as something normal, as described in the previous paragraph. Following the current regulation, the assessment of the eligibility of candidates for members of the Supervisory Boards shall be issued by the Supervisory Board in its old composition. The decision shall be issued on the basis of an assessment prepared by the Compliance Service. The bank can hire this job, but it can be part of the bank itself, as in the NLB case. As long as we have such a system, the bank will choose its own supervisors, the old supervisory councils will choose new ones. There's a clear conflict of interest here. The Compliance Service will be able to issue a positive assessment on the basis of a new member's counter-service so that it does not trouble certain disputed behaviours of the old. The same is true for the members of the Supervisory Board, which we are taking a final decision on. It is also in their interest to cover up their own affairs and sins. Thus, we do not really see how a system in which old supervisors confirm the suitability of new ones can lead to better supervision on the basis of an assessment of the bank's service itself under supervision. At the same time, we note that Banka Slovenije has always had the opportunity to prohibit the performance of the functions of members of the supervisory boards or of the bank administrations of the first Janša government, when the bulk of the bad loans became available. It is clear that the global capitalist crisis is not a result of banking crime and all relevant studies, in particular the work of Professor Prašnik from the Faculty of Economics, clearly show that most credits were not spent on Taiwanese credits but were spent on investment in the core activity. It is known, for example, that the credit capacity in road transport increased by 100 % between 2006 and 2008. This was a typical example of mine effect. Investors are looking for extra profits when expected returns are raised, investors are investing in increasingly risky project projects, doing so increasingly speculatively in anticipation of increasing returns. This is not a matter of individual morality, but in such a system it is somehow rational to act as a capitalist or manager, which is catastrophic for society as a whole and for capitalism itself. What I want to say is not that Banka Slovenije, by evaluating a member of the administrations and supervisory boards, could completely prevent such development, but that supervisors operating in Banka Slovenije could carry out their function significantly better if there was no built-in conflict of interest that the new supervisors would be appointed by an old supervisory committee, thus essentially creating a network in the banking sector. A real shift would have been better if the link had been interrupted by another social group that has an interest in ending it and being organised in it. That is why last year, as I said before, when the Banks Act was being considered, we proposed that the right of employees in banks to be appointed members of the Supervisory Board and also to appoint a Labour Director be returned. The collectives of employees would appoint their members to the posts, i.e. those employed in banks as well as representatives of labour organisations and trade unions. This would result in a combination of supervisors who would have a review of the situation in banks and supervisors who could not be bullied by employers or political parties because they would be behind them by a union and a collective of employees. This would restore the right of employees in banks to other workers in Slovenia or other employees in banks across Europe, such as Sweden, Austria and Germany, in short, where the system of co-management has been developed and is functioning. These are also the principles and objectives that we would like to implement with our proposal for a law on the participation of workers in management. As for this law, I repeat, it is a pity that the coalition has turned it back so easily because it responds to the real social problem that is taking place here and now before our eyes.

The requirement for a prior authorisation to act as a member of the supervisory board of the bank will certainly help to increase the transparency, rationality and systematicness of the procedures for the selection of supervisors and the stability of the later functioning of the supervisory board. Christian Democrats are proposing a procedural improvement in the method of appointment, which we consider to be positive. At the same time, we have announced or have already submitted a supplement to the appointment of members of the supervisory boards of banks in a substantive sense. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u59	Good afternoon. The position of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre will be presented by mag. Dušan Verbich.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u60	Thank you for your word, Vice President. Dear Secretary of State, Members and Members of Parliament, a very nice greeting! In the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, we can conclude that such a prepared law could not do so. This method of implementation is not clearly defined, nor is there a transitional period. With regard to the proposed content, however, it should be pointed out that in the Banking Act it did not provide for a prior authorisation of a member of the Supervisory Board for any bank established in the Republic of Slovenia. The Bank of Slovenia, however, has the competence, as has already been said, to provide an ex post assessment of the suitability of a member of the supervisory board of the bank. Thus, Banka Slovenije may, in accordance with Article 57 of the Banking Act 2 to verify that a member of the supervisory board of the bank fulfils the conditions and the obligations set out in Article 55 of the same law, decide to conduct an oral interview to which a member of the supervisory board of the bank is invited to present in more detail the circumstances which BS considers appropriate for the assessment of compliance with these conditions. In accordance with Article 273 of the same law, the Bank of Slovenia is also competent to prohibit, in certain cases, the management of the function of a member of the supervisory board of the Bank. In the case of banks considered to be important institutions, including the New Ljubljana Bank, ex post evaluation of the suitability of members of the Supervisory Board is also within the competence of the European Central Bank. The Bank itself must have a system in place in accordance with the Banking Act 2 whereby, after the appointment of members of the Authority's management, it carries out the process of assessing their suitability. With regard to the proposal, the Government points out that the proposal for this law is incomplete, since it does not specify who or what procedure to issue the authorisation. These issues are essential for assessing the content of the law. We have already studied the possibility of pre-licensing members of the supervisory boards of banks in the Republic of Slovenia when adopting Banking Act 2. In this regard, it should be pointed out that in the countries to which we are so eager to compare ourselves and to sum up their legal arrangements, such as Germany and Austria, they do not know this institution. It certainly seems pointless for banks, with responsible owners in the Republic of Slovenia, to carry out licensing procedures for members of the supervisory boards. However, this must be an obligation for members of the board, and this is also the way we are regulated in the Banking Act 2. Banks in the Republic of Slovenia must have qualified supervisors, and this must be primarily the duty and right of owners, and these Bank of Slovenia and the European Central Bank may, upon appointment, verify that they comply with all the conditions laid down under the Banking Act, in the event that they are not resolved and immediately initiated by appropriate procedures as laid down in the Banking Act 2. On the basis of the explanation given, the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre does not support the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Banking Act tabled by the Group of Non-Aligned Members.

Such thinking was finally also seen in the treatment of the parent body, where, in fact, there were no large-scale objections or strong objections. It is, however, a fact that, even through the debate on the Committee, it has been shown that, already in the existing law, these products can be classified as a category subject to a lower value added tax rate. Therefore, it is not necessary to amend the law, but a sub-legislative act that the Government can draw up, and thus avoid those issues that have been raised, in order to enforce the law in the middle of a tax period, and not least by a regulation to make it more practical to approach those who have already acquired hygiene deposits at the general tax rate. These are questions that must certainly be cleared before the transition is made. And by a regulation or a sub-legislative act adopted by the Government, this is easier and easier than by amending the law. The fact is that if this change is already made, it is also necessary to ensure that these savings, if we can say so, are not in the pockets of traders and intermediaries, but in fact consumers, due to a smaller value added tax. The representative of the Union of Consumers also pointed out that to this end it would be necessary to organise a large-scale action to expose all those traders who, instead of a reduced tax, would actually introduce a higher contribution, which they then consider to be the income of the trader, rather than a benefit to the buyer. That is why we believe that the law on the Committee has not been adopted and that there is actually a possibility that this could be regulated by a sub-law, that the Government should nevertheless examine this proposal and prepare a proposal for a solution that can reduce the cost of living for women, but at the same time look at the definition in this sub-legislative act, or that really all the articles that are classified in the lower value added tax rate are being judged and, perhaps, some would not have judged in the general tax rate and would have even balanced that gap if only a transition and enforcement were to be made for a lower tax rate for hygiene devices used by women. Thank you.

There are currently a number of entities among the taxpayers for tax clearance, from which a reasonable policy should not require the use of tax receipts. The funds collected from these events are intended mainly for the purchase of fire-fighting equipment, which is extremely expensive, and fire-fighting vehicles. It is precisely because of one such event that, after the current law, voluntary firefighting companies are also liable for online cash registers. The cost and administrative burden of tax receipts is simply too high for a voluntary fire company. Firefighting companies do not have employees and nor are they qualified to handle these devices, and the penalties for irregularities at risk are high. We are threatened by a reduction in the number of charity events, which will result in a reduction in the revenues of firefighters, all of which will be detrimental to human security and property. They're companies and they're acting non-profit. The company can only carry out that complementary gainful activity related to its purpose and objectives. In particular, smaller companies do not have the appropriate financial and administrative capacity to ensure the operation of tax warehouses. Their introduction, however, makes it disproportionately difficult to obtain financial resources. As an example, let us mention a mountain society that organizes a mountain trip for its members once a year. Such a company will have to establish a system of tax clearance of accounts as a result of one mountain trip. That doesn't make any sense. The same applies to all other companies operating in different fields. Such a measure is not reasonable and is not proportionate. The introduction of tax registers for companies makes it difficult to connect citizens and further increases social apathy. The reasons why the State may not be reasonable on the basis of restrictions on the operation and acquisition of funds in society. The introduction of tax boxes for small farms in the pursuit of complementary activities also entails a disproportionate burden on them. The complementary activity of farms often means preserving different traditions, products, important handicrafts and other skills. It increases the supply of local products and consequently enriches the lives of our local community, while giving farmers the opportunity to further develop and transfer knowledge to new generations. As a result of the introduction of tax boxes for small farms, we can expect the abandonment of complementary activities and consequently a significant reduction in creativity in the local field. Therefore, colleagues and colleagues, Christian Democrats propose that, for the first time, farms carrying out complementary activities with less than 15,000 euros of annual income, secondly, all voluntary firefighting companies and thirdly, other companies with less than 10,000 euros of annual income will be burdened by the use of tax receipts. In addition, we propose to abolish the sanctioning of customers who do not take over or retain the account for the goods or services purchased immediately after leaving the premises. In our view, a penalty of €40 is an unreasonable intervention in the position of buyers and consumers. Colleagues and colleagues, we propose and ask that we open up a high-quality debate, a constructive debate and a second reading of the law to optimize it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u77	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, Mag. Matei Vraničnik Erman, State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u78	Mr Vice-President, thank you for the floor. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The government's proposed amendment to the Law on tax clearance of accounts opposes and proposes to the National Assembly that you find that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. We introduced tax clearances four months ago. The first partial data analysis for the period from January to the end of March is known. This analysis shows the first positive effects, which, of course, cannot yet be fully confirmed or said to be the only effects, since for more than those who are liable to confirm accounts, the effect will not be shown until the following year, as it is for persons who are not taxable in the value added tax system. The basic regulation, i.e. the Law on tax clearance of accounts, introduced, in accordance with the origins for the preparation of the law and the requirements of the interested public, the obligation to tax confirmation of invoices for all debtors who are obliged to issue invoices for the goods sold or for services provided in accordance with the rules on value added tax and receive payments in cash. Exceptions are narrowly defined, in a way where control itself can be carried out on the basis of other information. The proposed law to exempt voluntary fire companies, companies whose income is less than EUR 10 000 last year and farms from carrying out complementary activities, provided that the revenues of the previous financial year do not exceed EUR 15 000 under this Title, would still require those entities to issue an invoice in accordance with the rules governing value added tax, i.e. for each supply of goods or services rendered. The exemption would apply only to tax clearance of accounts. The question is whether this exemption is justified taking into account the origins at which the law is prepared and taking into account the principle of equal treatment of the debtors in the same or similar situation. At the same time, we must also ask ourselves whether, for all the entities mentioned by the applicant and which he wishes to exempt from the validation of accounts, the right administrative obstacle is only the validation of accounts or perhaps just the fact that the account has to be issued. In view of the fact that the fundamental administrative obstacle is the very obligation to issue accounts in some cases, we have prepared an amendment to the Regulation implementing the Value Added Tax Act, which aims at eliminating this administrative burden for certain smaller debtors, but at the same time does not unduly distinguish business entities from one another. In particular, small business operators have repeatedly pointed out that the economic activity that companies and similar non-profit organisations can carry out under the applicable rules can constitute unfair competition for them. The questions relating to the additional activity on the farm are also addressed by a new proposal from the Cadastrian Income Law, which will be discussed below. If I conclude, we estimate that the proposed law does not address the administrative obstacles of the debtors it mentions in an appropriate manner and that these debtors can be better dealt with by other measures, while the comprehensive amendment of the Law on tax clearance of accounts will make sense once the system of clearance of accounts itself is operational and we will also have a comprehensive analysis on the table of the effects of this system, which we are now introducing during the introductory period. Thank you very much.

With this bill from the government tax system, Christian Democrats want to abolish some of the most unreasonable provisions. The bill, which is before us, is also supported by a number of local communities, to list some of them: Municipality of Črenšovci, Municipality of Slovenj Gradec, Municipality of Škofljica, Municipality of Ormož, Municipality of Rogatec, Municipality of Šmarje pri Jelšah, Municipality of Loška dolina, Municipality of Rogaška Slatina, Municipality of Šoštanj, Municipality of Pivka, Municipality of Kamnik, Municipality of Lukovica, Municipality of Železniki, Municipality of Ribnica and support will still be available. We hope that the congregations will also be joined by all the Parliamentary groups of the National Assembly and will be supported by law. In Novi Slovenija, we will of course support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on tax clearance of accounts.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u114	Thank you so much for saying that. A number of questions have emerged in the debate so far, requiring answers. This time, too, the debate is broader than the content of the proposed law, but first I will focus only on issues relating to the content of the law. The law in force in the legislative procedure included, as Mr Horvat quoted, two exceptions. One relates to foreign debtors, because they work abroad, we do not have to interfere with our jurisdiction, and the other with continuing supplies where payment and traffic control is provided in a different way. Despite this, we have always advocated and still advocate that the system of clearance of accounts should apply to all accounts to be issued in connection with the supply of goods or services. It is two conditions, an invoice must be issued and it must go for the supply of goods and services. In connection with fire companies, societies, NGOs, voluntary organisations, you have used different terms, these are activities that do not mainly represent the transport of goods and services. Collection of voluntary contributions, no obligation to confirm or issue invoices – even under the applicable legislation. Donations — no obligation to issue an account. The obligation to issue an invoice under the current legislation which was in force before the entry into force of the Law on the Tax attestation of Accounts was applicable to these entities in the event that they made the delivery of goods or services. For a specific example of fire companies, this may also mean the delivery of a calendar at the end of the year or any supply on a firehouse. For mountain society or pensioner society this also means co-financing of transport related to the trip. And what we learned when these societies began to turn to us, that the Law on the Tax attestation of Accounts hurt their lives. We have found that they do not respect the Value Added Tax Act and that they do not issue invoices for these things where they should issue accounts. Therefore, at the Ministry, we asked ourselves whether it was right for us to call these NGOs, these NGOs, you have been doing the wrong thing so far, you have to issue the accounts and then you have to tax them. Of course, transactions from which value added tax does not have to be paid because they are legally exempt from it and should be invoiced. And when we asked ourselves this question, we found that the main administrative burden of these organisations, especially the small ones, is that they have to issue accounts at all, so we went to the rules on the implementation of the value added tax law, and we proposed that such organisations for the supply of smaller annual volumes be exempt from the obligation to issue an account. In the public debate we received a more or less positive response, the only comment was on the definition of occasionality, that we should define the frequency in more detail, that comment will be examined in the coming days, and by the end of this month we will also implement an amendment to the Regulation which will remove this administrative obstacle for the smallest debtors. The solution proposed through the proposal of the law, however, distinguishes, without any legal basis, between different activities. Already within NGOs, it sets different criteria. Furthermore, it imposes essentially different obligations between NGOs, which, to a certain extent, provide goods and services and which small entrepreneurs claim to be unfair to them to a certain extent. And in the solutions we accept, we need to find the right balance between all these different solutions. We believe that the regulation in the current law on tax clearance of accounts is appropriate. We also believe that the administrative burden proposed through the amendment of the rules on the implementation of value added tax is justified and is essentially addressing the key problems that have been pointed out by small undertakings over the past two or three months. I agree that this solution must be implemented quickly so that we do not give these debtors unnecessary costs. Now, for some of the broader statements that require a comment or a response. The concept of the grey economy is very broad and, unfortunately, it is interpreted separately. The emerging forms of the grey economy are huge and cash business is only one area that is particularly sensitive to the emergence of the grey economy. With the system of tax confirmation of accounts, we are not pretending to address all the emerging forms of the grey economy, but only one, which the tax administration, with intensive field work, has seen since mid-2013 further intensively. It is essentially about modifying data in invoices issued or issuing accounts for lower amounts than are actually agreed prices for delivery of goods and services. Most of the countries that have introduced tax clearances or tax receipts have started from scratch, so we have no confirmation, we introduce tax receipts and we have big financial effects. We built the system gradually, firstly called virtual tax boxing, the estimated effect of the circus 100 million a year. To this effect, the additional introduction of tied accounts, around 20 to 30 million additional effects, and the estimated additional financial impact of tax validation of accounts around 75 million. Data for the first three months indicate that this estimated effect will actually be achieved. However, this is not all the effect that we create by fighting against the grey economy. It is necessary to add the effects that we have in the area of combating employment and working in black, the effects of sanctioning the unauthorised abuse of the institution of a single entrepreneur, which is the task of financial administration together with the Inspectorate for the Work of the Republic of Slovenia, the activities we carry out in relation to the identification of unexplained origin of assets, transfers or transactions in more tax-friendly areas. Over the past year and a half, a huge number of tax inspectors have been involved in the field and the additional impact of an individual inspector has been relatively small in this sector of the grey economy in the field of cash management. The system of tax clearance of accounts allows for a greater impact with less presence on the ground, allowing it to go to the ground precisely where data shows that there is some unexplained practice or some unreasonable data coming out, allowing us to make more effective use of the number of inspectors available to us and reduce the number of inspections to be made, makes it possible to make random checks, allowing less stress on the taxpayers themselves. The costs we have with the tax inspectors are the same or are dealing with the area of the grey economy in cash operations or are now being diverted to the grey economy in the area of non-institution, i.e. the control of rainforests, or the control of work and employment in black, both activities require an intensive presence in the field. However, this is an area of activity that we do not mention in this report, and this report focuses only on those who are obliged to confirm the accounts. And data also show that the number of business entities rose between December and today, rising in all segments of business operators. That's on the back of the report we issued yesterday. With regard to when the Government adopted the opinion, the government adopted the opinion yesterday, apparently there was a lapsus that I had not even warned you about before that was actually an old date used on a form on which such opinions are usually prepared. And one last thing. Revenue from the budget in the first four months of this year. Unfortunately, the data should be analysed in a little more detail, not only to compare the two figures that will appear last year this year. It should be borne in mind that the obligation to pay excise duties and VAT is due on the last day of the month; in this year it was the last day of Saturday in April; in accordance with the law of the debtor it is not necessary to pay the tax liability on the last working day but to settle the tax liability on the first day of the next day. And that's what happened. The 60 million tax applicable to the April tax period was settled at the beginning of May. However, since we are recording revenue after the cash flow, they cannot be recorded during the month of April. The commentator also does not take into account that there was a high tax on legal persons' income in last year in April, with over 120 million in this amount. Why? Because the withdrawals in 2014 were too low. On the basis of the newly calculated tax, new corporate tax advances were established, valid from May to April of this year, which were higher and more consistent with the actual data, such as those reported at the end of March by companies liable to corporate income tax, and therefore were these payments, net payments in this April only, under quotations, 22 million and not 120, as in the previous year. Unfortunately, we cannot obtain data on the volume of VAT from the purchases that the taxpayers had to make in order to adapt to themselves the information system for tax clearance of accounts. This is not even so important because the information we provide in the report, which I keep talking about, speaks about the net amount of VAT, i.e. payments reduced for deductions of the entry tax. And since we have analysed taxpayers who are taxable during this period, all of these VAT taxpayers can also deduct VAT paid on the purchase of such equipment as input VAT, and during this period of additional VAT revenue cannot be recorded. The extent or number of taxable persons engaged in a partially exempt, partially taxed activity is negligible in this segment of the debtors who have to confirm the accounts. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u148	Mr Vice-President, thank you for the floor. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The Government forwarded a written opinion to the Proposal for a Law amending the Act on the determination of cadastral income. We suggest that you do not support the law for two key reasons. The first reason is that the draft law not only regulates the extension of the transitional solution currently in force, but rather regulates it in an inappropriate way. It is proposed that the administratively complex way of gradually increasing cadastral income be reinstated, although the proposal to amend the law should only address the determination of cadastral income for 2017. It is understood from the text of the Act that, in order to determine the cadastral income for 2017, the amounts of cadastral income per parcel should also be recalculated on the basis of the new method for 2014, 2015 and 2016, so that they can then apply the new transitional period for 2017 at all. This doubt expressed on the proposal for a law stems from a comparison of the first paragraph of the proposal to amend Article 15 and Article 15(1) of the Act on the determination of cadastral income. The proposed solution is very difficult to implement administratively. The system’s contractors, the Geodetic Authority of the Republic of Slovenia, are subject to disproportionate administrative burdens, and in the view of the Government, it does not even solve the problem posed by the proposer as a target, since the proposal does not necessarily mean maintaining the cadastral income level of 2013 but could even increase the cadastral income for 2017. Another reason why the Government does not support the law is that we have already prepared a proposal for a new cadastral income law, and on Monday we have a final coordination meeting with NGOs, and we expect it to be submitted to the National Assembly for consideration at a June or July session. The new bill introduces a new method of determining cadastral income, which brings a more transparent, simple and stable system, which will be tested every three years. It will be available on aggregated data of the economic account and will be determined for the whole country. The proposal for a new law also addresses the objective pursued by the proposer. In addition, it brings important administrative simplifications to certain groups of debtors who, according to their current income system, need to identify their activities on the basis of accounting management, as the new bill provides the basis for regulating the flat-rate determination of KD also for special intensive cultures. It also allows for the regulation of further administrative simplifications under the income system, which is not possible solely on the basis of the extension of the current cadastral income law. It therefore does not support the proposal and considers that the new proposal for a law will also address the objective pursued by the proposal of the proposer. In addition, it provides other positive effects to the government's proposal for a law. We therefore propose that the National Assembly of the proposed parliamentary law does not support it as appropriate for further consideration. Thank you very much.

In 2014, a new law on the identification of cadastral income was adopted, setting the cadastral income and the flat-rate assessment of bee income for 2014, 2015 and 2016 as set out for 2013. The purpose of the amendment to the law that we are considering today is to extend this state for another year, that is, for 2017. The proposal for a change of law comes to the National Assembly after the proposal for a new Cadastrian Income Regulation prepared by the Ministry of Finance raised a lot of dust in the public eye. Under the new law proposed by the Ministry, cadastral income would in some cases rise by more than a hundred, including a thousand percent. Representatives of agricultural organisations have made it clear that such a law is inappropriate, especially since it comes at a time when Slovenian agriculture has affected one of the worst, in fact the worst in years. Last year, we witnessed the decline in redemption prices, which have made the social situation of Slovenian farmers dramatically worse, and in the forests there have been a proliferation of underground areas. We have a cold this year, and we're not even halfway through the year. Since cadastral income is also linked to the right to an island supplement, scholarships and other social transfers, a drastic increase in cadastral income would mainly affect poor farmers. We must be aware that the cadastral income is not only a mechanism for charging the state budget, but that its function is also to force farmers into processing the land. Where cadastral income is unrealistically highly determined only on the basis of land ratings and does not take into account the socioeconomic situation of farmers, it may force landowners to abandon agricultural activity and sell land. However, we must also be aware that years ago the profession found that the applicable methodology for determining cadastral income was no longer appropriate. This fact is not opposed by the state-of-the-art organisation or, in fact, by the proposer himself. We need a new methodology, so it would be more appropriate to adopt a new Cadastrian Income Identification Act with a methodology that would be agreed by both the profession and the policy, taking into account all comments made by housing organisations. We obviously need a new methodology. However, provisions could also be introduced into the law itself to protect the socially most vulnerable farmers. The new law that we are considering today prolongs the existing situation for another year, and in the light of the recent accidents that have affected Slovenian agriculture, such an extension does not seem to be wrong. Nevertheless, it has long been known that we need a new methodology for calculating cadastral income and that it would nevertheless be better to adopt a new law on the determination of cadastral income. The extension of the current situation every year and every few years has a long-term negative impact on the agricultural situation, as it introduces uncertainty when farmers for each of the following years do not know what cadastral income they will have to pay. That is why we need a solution that will, as has already been said, compromise between the profession, farmers and politics and allow a fair and socially sustainable determination of cadastral income. In addition, the novel not only prolongs the existing state but also changes it, although this is not supposed to be the purpose of the law. At the same time, it changes the way the cadastral income is determined backwards. Therefore, in the United Left, we will be abstained from voting on this law at first reading. Thank you.

Above all, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has decided that this data in the Earth disaster will cease to be maintained. In addition, the section notes that the method in force before is no longer suitable for the modernisation of the cadastral income calculations which have been obsolete and significantly underestimated in the light of the actual economic situation. It was therefore necessary to develop and implement a new method based on new data on the quality of agricultural and forestry land, i.e. credits and growth coefficients, taking into account the information on the more detailed type of land used for all agricultural land in the Republic of Slovenia by the Ministry responsible for agriculture. As regards the solution to the new cadastral income drawn up by that Government, it should be pointed out that it was based on a decision of 30. 8. 2013 established working group consisting of representatives of the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food, the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Environment and Space, experts in the field of agrarian economy, some professional institutions and also interest associations such as the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia, the Association of Slovenia and the Union of Farmers of Slovenia, and tasked it with verifying the appropriateness of the new method and preparing proposals for better solutions. In anticipation of the findings of the Working Group, a new amendment to the Cadastrian Income Identification Act was introduced in 2014, which provided that cadastral income and flat-rate income assessment on bee bees for 2014, 2015 and 2016 are determined as set out for 2013 and that the annual calculation of cadastral income for those years is not carried out. This has frozen the cadastral income to the level set out in the amendment for 2013. In view of the state of play entered, a cadastral income shall be determined on the basis of a government proposal for a law which shall apply until the end of this year. We do not believe that an extension of the transitional period is necessary as the Government has prepared a new law on cadastral income. The extension of the old method of determining cadastral income does not show a proper assessment, a government law should be in force, which will mainly be a technical law, which will regulate the method of determining cadastral income by which, on the basis of the available data and in the most understandable way, it would be necessary to determine as much as possible the real income and thus comparable treatment of all natural persons. A proposal for a government law proposes solutions for the calculation of cadastral income based on aggregate data from the economic account for agriculture and forestry. The main agricultural activity will also include intensive production of intensive crops under the new Cadastrian Income Act, namely the production of strawberries, asparagus, intensive gardening, snail farming and the like, so these crops will not need to be invoiced after the new crop, nor will they need to be taxed. Catastrophe income shall be determined in the production capacity classes for agricultural land on the basis of the credit points and for forest land on the basis of the growth coefficient. The correction of cadastral income is maintained due to the limited possibility of production in the areas of specific farming and forest management regimes. Catastrophe income and flat-rate income estimates on bee stumps shall be determined every three years on the basis of the previous five-year average of the economic account data. The applicant submits that the main reason for their proposed law was that during the month of April there were colds which caused significant crop damage, mainly in vegetables and agriculture. The injury will affect the calculation of KD for 2016 and therefore the extension of the transitional period for this area will not be beneficial and no extension of the transitional period is necessary. As far as gardeners with a majority of less than €200 of cadastral income are concerned, this is 68%, and this cadastral income is not accounted for or taken into account in income. The damage to vineyards and orchards will be tackled by specific measures, in addition to the government's proposal for a cadastral income law introduced by the transitional period, so that it will not be completely changed for 2017, taking into account only 40% of the calculated amount of cadastral income. On average, this year’s cadastral income will be even slightly lower than in 2016. In view of the above, the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre will be opposed to the fact that the proposal for a law amending the Cadastrian Income Recognition Act is suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u212	Dear President, honourable Members, honourable Members! Allow me a brief explanation. You have before you the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the law on the promotion of coherent regional development. The amendments are proposed to improve solutions that have proven to be weak or difficult to implement in the past. The more effective promotion of coherent regional development has been our basic guide and the final objective of the proposed changes. Let's go in one order. The draft law addresses current issues concerning regional development agencies, in particular their ownership structure of the deletion from the RRA records and their public function in the regions. In the ownership structure of the RRA, there will no longer be less than a third of the establishment of one municipality in the framework of public interest, and the institution remains a majority public property. The proposal provides for the regional development agencies to elect a Council of the region with a call for proposals giving priority to institutions with a higher proportion of the public impact. We propose simplifications in replacing a member of the development council of the region so that the Council, by a majority of the votes of all members, can replace an individual member if the replacement is proposed by a body that has also proposed a member. In addition, municipalities with a development coefficient under the Community Financing Act of less than 0.9 are added to border problems. Come in. The relationship to the special pomurical development law is clarified. A problem area with high unemployment will not be able to be defined as an area receiving aid under the post-moral development law. New tasks in the area of promoting and developing entrepreneurship and the culture of innovation, the implementation of regional programmes for internationalisation of the economy and spatial planning at regional level shall be added to the development tasks of the countries carried out at regional level. It is deleted that arrangements for the development of the regions shall be concluded for four years. The agreement for the development of the regions will be valid until the period of duration of the projects included or replaced by a new agreement. We further propose that the smaller transfers between the items of the business and financial plan of the Slovenian Regional Development Fund be decided by the Minister responsible for regional development and not by the Government. In addition, it is proposed that the fund is owned by a fund that ensures the operation of the fund and provides for a way to invest the fund’s free funds. Next, In the case of indigenous national communities and the Roma community, the proposal removes the uncertainty about the type of incentives provided by the Slovenian Regional Development Fund and the State budget. The State budget provides grants and the Fund with reimbursements, as well as grants for indigenous national communities. With regard to cohesion regions, we propose deleting the provision that, in the implementation of the programmes of the European Union or parts thereof carried out only within one cohesion region, the performance function be transferred to the unit of the competent national authority for regional policy in this cohesion region. Regional State aid and regional policy emergency measures shall be subject to a more detailed allocation procedure. In addition, some legal editorial corrections are proposed due to legislation adopted in the European Union and Slovenia following the entry into force of the USSR 2. Finally, let me stress that the effects of all the measures of the law adopted in 2011 and amended in 2012 cannot yet be fully evaluated. This is why we have not proposed greater interventions to the law. However, the changes will allow, in our opinion, faster and easier implementation of coherent regional development programmes. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u213	Thank you very much. We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats, for which Mr Jožef Horvat is responsible, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u214	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues, distinguished Mr Secretary of State with colleagues! The government novel of the Law on Promoting Harmonised Regional Development brings some changes to the regime, but for the whole system there are no major shifts. With regard to uncertainties in the management of regional development agencies, the law proposes to elect a regional development agency of the Council of the Region for a programming period, by means of a public call led by a municipality from which the Council of the Region or a municipality designated by the Council of the Region is chaired. We find it inappropriate to propose that the municipality from which the President of the Council of the Region could lead a public call for the selection of the contractor for the performance of public regional tasks. In our view, there is no reason for such a privilege for the congregation from which the President comes. In the current regime, the Minister for the performance of these tasks shall be the authority of the regional development agencies. In New Slovenia, we see no point in introducing change, as the solution to date has been appropriate in our view. Article 24 of the bill provides for defined border areas. Enlargement of border areas is proposed. In New Slovenia, we note that the funding foreseen in this area is already declining for existing areas, so the expansion of border areas does not bring solutions. In the past, it has proved that the congregations had the greatest problems at the border. Let's just remember the migrant crisis. But we are not idealists, so we do not have the hope that this expansion of border areas would be followed by a budget. As regards financing for the development of areas where the Roma community lives, the Fund has only sources of return, such as guarantees, loans and so on. In the opinion of the Government, however, return resources are not suitable to address the Roma problem. The proposal for a law therefore clearly provides for financial incentives in the State budget for faster development of areas where the Roma community lives. The Government's argument that return resources are not suitable to address the Roma issue and that grants must be fixed for them raises the question of why Roma need special treatment and special determination of grants. Nevertheless, the law also brings some positive changes. Among the development tasks of countries in the region in the public interest, the proposal adds: promotion and development of entrepreneurship and culture of innovation, implementation of regional programmes of internationalisation of the economy and spatial planning at regional level. That's what we welcome. The law also simplifies that transfers between items of the business and financial plan of the Public Fund of the Republic of Slovenia for Regional Development and Rural Development will be subject to decision by the Minister. The proposal for a law specifies in more detail that candidates for representatives of municipalities in the development council of the region are proposed by the municipal council. During the term of office, the Development Council may, on a proposal from the Municipal Council, replace a member who no longer fulfils the conditions, for example, his municipal function shall cease. In Novi Slovenija, we will not oppose the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Promotion of Coherent Regional Development.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u218	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. Dear Mr Secretary of State, representatives of the Government, colleagues and colleagues! The proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Promotion of Harmonised Regional Development, according to the proposer, brings changes and additions in two areas, if I am generalising. First of all, the system solutions which have proven to be weak or difficult to implement after the entry into force of the law in 2012 or are inconsistent with the amended European legislation should be improved. Second, they cited the rationalisation of administrative procedures, as already mentioned, in order to facilitate the implementation of the law and additional savings in the use of public funds. We believe that some of the objectives of amending the law are correct, because in recent years there has been a constant focus on the problems in this area. We also observe questionable solutions. However, on general matters, I would not be taking your time now, but in the context of our position, I will focus mainly on the content that is related to the issue of the economic basis of indigenous national communities. In the context of this law, I should also like to begin by thanking my colleague, Mr Lajo, who has already pointed out some of the content and problems. Initially, I would like to point out that we support the proposed solution to address the confusion and difficulties in financing the development of the Roma community or the areas where its representatives reside, including the provision that incentives for that community should also be set out in the State budget. In accordance with the legislation, the text of the amendment was also sent to the competent organisations of the Italian and Hungarian national communities, which forwarded their opinions and proposals to supplement the law in terms of indigenous national communities on this important content. The above-mentioned opinions reiterate the systemic problem that has been emerging in this area for more than a decade and which has so far not been successfully addressed by any government in order to effectively address such incentives in order to ensure the economic basis of indigenous national communities. The current amendment of the law is therefore a new opportunity for this systemic problem to finally begin to be resolved, and from this place I am also again calling on the proposer, namely the Government of the Republic of Slovenia or the competent ministry, to listen to the proposals of the national community. This is, among other things, its commitment, which is enshrined in the Coalition Treaty.
