The proposed amendment to the law should take into account our proposals, since among other principles it is stated that the complementary proposal will take into account the principle of subsidiarity, which should also mean, in the context of Article 64 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, that decisions on the creation of an economic base are taken by the indigenous national community within or in agreement with the national community itself. National community organisations in their opinions and in other letters to the Government point out that the funds provided are allocated to enterprises or entities in the national mixed area, which is not the case, but in recent years it has proved that this does not solve the problem of creating the economic basis of the national communities themselves. I would like to point out that the creation of the economic foundation of the national communities was necessary or that it would be necessary, in particular, because of their existence and development, not because of any other circumstances or reasons. One of the above-mentioned letters to the Government is written by the umbrella organisations, and I quote: "The economic basis of the national mixed area does not necessarily coincide with the economic basis of the national communities and the development of the first does not necessarily mean the development of the latter, while the created economic basis of the national community certainly contributes to the development of the national mixed area." We add that there will be no mistake that the comprehensive development of the area where the national communities live is very important and necessary, but the economic basis of the national communities themselves must also be created. In doing so, however, it is necessary to find appropriate systemic solutions which, unfortunately, have not yet been or have not been found. On the basis of the above, we propose that the provision of amending the law relating to indigenous national communities should be supplemented by clearly defining, in addition to the development of so-called national mixed areas, the importance of development or the creation of an economic basis for national communities or activities for which money is consistently provided under the national budget. By the way, this is guaranteed in the adopted budgets for 2016 and 2017 to a certain extent. In the context of the explanations of the articles of the law which are being amended, the applicant makes it clear that the purchases from the privatisation of the social property which, in the past, were the only sources under the law in the area of the creation of an economic base have practically dried up. By the way, systemically, as I have already pointed out, this use has not been properly set up. Therefore, the proposer proposes that all other existing options should be used for this purpose in the future. We agree with that. It is also necessary to implement a provision that the State may authorise the self-governance of the national community to carry out certain tasks in this area under national jurisdiction. I will not refer here to a specific text of the Constitution which gives a clear basis for this, but the law is so formulated that it already covers this text. On the basis of this community, they may establish this fund for the development of their own economic activity or for this appropriate institution, which, in terms of transparency, will have to operate in accordance with the principles of other such institutions of national interest. Finally, a number of consultations have been organised in the competent ministry over the last period in search of solutions, where, at least according to our information, they have not come up with satisfactory solutions that would fully address this issue from the point of view of the national communities. This is also why, in the context of the proposed amendments to the Law on the Promotion of Harmonised Regional Development, a system of creating the economic foundation of national communities is being built up meaningfully, so that their proposals and views are finally taken into account. The proposal to amend the law will also be discussed at a meeting of the Commission for National Communities. On the basis of the opinion of the competent organisations of the national communities, we will propose concrete solutions or amendments aimed at effectively creating the economic basis of the national communities. In doing so, we are counting on the constructive cooperation of the executive branch. Formal amendments to the law are not necessary, so we expect a sincere intention for substantive upgrade so far in our view of an inefficient system. The amendment of the law represents a good opportunity, as I have already said, for this, so we will support the conclusion that the amendment of the law is appropriate for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u220	Dear Presidents, colleagues from the Ministry, colleagues, colleagues! The Act on coherent regional development was adopted already in 1999 to promote coherent regional development, allocate development incentives and criteria for identifying areas with specific development problems. Subsequent changes, additions, last one, the fifth was adopted in 2012, mainly due to the urgent resolution of situations that have brought fragmentation and opacity of the regime. Today's bill pursues two key objectives. Firstly, improvements in systemic solutions that have proven to be weak or difficult to implement since the law was enacted in 2012 and will not facilitate the achievement of the objectives of the law. Secondly, rationalisation of administrative procedures that will facilitate implementation and additional savings in the use of public funds. We believe that the adoption of the law is necessary because of the need to preserve the situation of certain RRAs, which would otherwise have been deleted from the register, which would be a severe sanction for the relatively leakage of infrastructure and programmes in the area of regional development, but not least that would have consequences for the development of the regions. It is also important to take into account the fact that development agencies will also have to carry out their functions and objectives in the future with less funding. The practice over the years shows that we have learned to draw on funds for regional development, but we must also show that we can also include projects that offer long-term sustainable models and will also include a greater share of our own funding resources. Let me mention the key changes to today's bill. The development tasks of the State in the region in the public interest are added: the promotion and development of entrepreneurship and cultural innovation, the implementation of regional programmes, the internationalisation of the economy, the development of competences for employees and spatial planning at regional level. It is further repealed that, within the framework of a public share in a regional development agency, none of the municipalities should have more than a third of the founding share and that, within a public establishment, the proportion of municipalities must be dispersed as a matter of urgency, since by the deadline set by the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Promotion of Harmonised Regional Development of 2012, until 28 July 2016, the ownership structure will not be recast in the five development regions in order to meet these two conditions, which should be deleted from the register, which would have had negative consequences, as previously said, for the development of those regions. The funding ratio for general development tasks at regional level of 60% of state resources with 40% of municipal funding for annual provision is changed. The criterion for the designation of municipalities belonging to border areas is supplemented by the inclusion of municipalities with a statutory development coefficient of less than 0,9 if they border border municipalities or municipalities directly bordering border municipalities. Due to the recently adopted Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Development Support to the Pomurska Region in the period 2010-2015, which extended the period of implementation of this Act for two years, the possibility of double eligibility of the Pomurje Development Region to regional incentives is eliminated by this proposal. Thus, in accordance with the specific pomura law and in accordance with the system law on the promotion of coherent regional development. At the same time, it is prevented by this special pomura law. As a region with the highest unemployment rate in the new financial perspective 2014-2020, certain measures would not be justified at all. The law also provides for a more detailed procedure for the implementation of regional State aid on the basis of a direct application. As a result of all of this, we will support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Promotion of Coherent Regional Development in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre. Thank you.

Finally, we have a law to promote coherent regional development. The law has a very significant title and, of course, we agree on the title. In preparing the basis for drawing up European funds from the new financial perspective 2014-2020, despite the call by the representative of the regions, you did not want to prepare two separate operational programmes, separate for the Eastern and Western cohesion regions, where it would be clear how much funding belongs to each region. We are talking about regions that are at very different levels of development. While the Western cohesion region is 97% of the European Union's development, the Eastern cohesion region is only 68% of the European Union's development. There is therefore a significant difference between regions. In doing so, it should be pointed out that there are still statistical regions within these two cohesion regions that point to a significant greater gap in development. We have the Central Slovenian region, which is at 116% of the European Union's development, while we have regions such as Pomurje, Zasavje, a karst area where development is even below 60%, according to the average of the European Union. Regions that do not reach 75% of the EU average are considered less developed regions under European cohesion policy. It is correct to recall, however, that in the negotiations for the funds of the new financial perspective, we have acquired an additional half billion euro at the expense of the less developed Eastern cohesion region. Today, however, it is a pity that it is part of the funds allocated to the eastern region and is known how much the eastern region will actually be allocated 1.2 billion. The Western region is allocated EUR 0.8 billion, while there is a difference, that is the value of EUR 1 billion for the whole of Slovenia, which, of course, can also theoretically mean that all of these funds will be allocated to the already over-developed Western region. And here we ask ourselves how this follows the principle of coherent regional development. Would it not be right to encourage coherent regional development in all decisions, or would it not be right to think about coherent regional development at all times and everywhere? Would it not be appropriate for more resources to be allocated to regions that are under-averagely developed, particularly those that do not reach even 75% of the average of development at European Union level? You have introduced tax receipts, and this before you have taken care of Slovenia's even coverage with the Internet, which means that individuals who want to carry out activities in individual areas are already on the start at a much worse starting point, but there you have forgotten the consistent regional development. It is right to play an important role in the capital. It should be noted, however, that Slovenia is not only Ljubljana. In particular, projects in areas that are fundamentally lagging behind from the EU average and also from the Slovenian average would be encouraged in the first place. In view of the many actions of this government coalition, in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we are asking whether we can even talk about coherent regional development today. Of course, we cannot go beyond the fact that some regional development agencies do not work as they should. Let us even recall the nonsense that the government of Borut Pahor has enjoyed in the past when, despite the existing development agency in the region, it established an additional so-called government project office. This same project office carried out a work that could be undertaken by a regional development agency. Of course, quickly after the establishment of this office, it was clear why this office was needed at all. Of course, it was only because of the interest of individuals or a narrower group of people. We can no longer afford the same in the future. If we focus on the proposal to amend the Law on the Promotion of Coherent Regional Development, we can see that the law brings a number of major changes. By amending the law, the individual development tasks of the State are transferred to the regional level in a certain way, or by amending the law the tasks performed in the region are extended in the public interest. You have thus added to the promotion and development of entrepreneurship and cultural innovation, the implementation of regional programmes for internationalisation of the economy, spatial planning at regional level, other similar regional activities, schemes and projects following the decision of the Ministry of Content and the agreement of the Ministry. We are, of course, wondering in what form and with what powers spatial planning is transferred to the regional level and whether this is coordinated with the competent ministries. We also wonder what are the so-called other similar regional activities, schemes and projects following a decision of the Ministry of Content and with the consent of the Ministry, and on what basis will the Ministry provide for these activities and projects. The word "can" in the law is completely inconclusive and inappropriate. We are also wondering whether deleting the provision on the urgent dispersed proportion of municipalities within the public institution's share of regional development agencies is the right way. It is right to stress the current situation of regional development agencies, which is not least exposed to the price of the proposed law. We have 12 regional development agencies, of which six public institutions, one institution and five companies with limited responsibility. At the same time, 14 other development institutions have been entered in the register, which together with regional development agencies carry out general development tasks in the region. Do we really need that many? Would not these twelve regional agencies be enough? Finally, they are financed at the expense of taxpayers. The share is covered by the Ministry and the share is covered by the municipalities. The law does not place the problem of coherent regional development on the right footing, nor are the solutions comprehensive. It contains some good solutions that we support. Some, however, are deeply misleading in their marriage. If this is intentional or not, of course it will be evident from the upcoming hearings and the debate on the law. Because of the very importance of coherent regional development, we hope that the law will be properly corrected through parliamentary procedure. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u231	Mrs Vojka Šergan has the floor to prepare Mr Ivan Škodnik and Janko Weber.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u232	Thank you for your word. Dear representatives of the Ministry of Economy, colleagues and colleagues! The coherent regional development law has substantive solutions, concrete effects in practice and public financial effects. Amendments and additions to the Harmonised Regional Development Act are proposed in two areas of improvement of systemic solutions that have proven to be weak or difficult to implement in 2012 after the implementation of the Harmonised Regional Development Act and will facilitate the achievement of the objectives of the law and rationalise administrative procedures that will facilitate implementation and additional savings in the use of public funds. Under the new law, an agreement will no longer be drawn up for the development of the regions for a period of four years, but these arrangements will be linked to the implementation of the operational programmes of European cohesion policy and budgetary planning and to the content of the projects involved. This is certainly a major step forward because all agencies have encountered difficulties in implementing European cohesion policy because it was planned for four years and projects could then have a longer period or a possibility of drawing on European cohesion funds. Then an agreement is also proposed – the agreement for the development of the region will apply until the period of duration of the projects involved or replacements with new ones. The draft law adequately regulates the deletion of the regional development agency from the register. Until the selection of a new regional development agency, it shall carry out the tasks of supporting the functioning of the Council and the Council of the Region of the Municipality, whose representative shall be the President of the Council of the Region, unless the Council of the Region designates another municipality. This has also proved in practice that it is important, given that some agencies have closed down in the past and even in the future, that an agency is to be terminated and will be terminated. The draft law provides for a detailed procedure for the granting of financial assistance in the event of urgent regional policy measures, where the aid is granted by the Ministry of Content and where it is granted by the State Fund of the Republic of Slovenia for Regional Development and Rural Development. It also provides for a detailed procedure for the award of regional aid without a call for tender on the basis of an application and a direct contract. By repealing the provision that none of the municipalities may have more than a third of the founder’s share in the regional development agency, the administrative burden in the municipalities shall be reduced. With regard to regional development agencies, the terminological uncertainty of the concept of a legal person in majority public ownership is also addressed, which is replaced by a more appropriate concept of a legal person with a majority public influence on the basis of the majority share of the subscribed capital or the founding share and with the majority influence of the municipalities within public influence. This is therefore not a different substantive solution from the present one, but merely a terminological ambiguity. A more flexible regulation of the decision-making process on minor transfers within the business and financial plan of the Regional Rural Development Public Fund is proposed; then transfers between the items of the Fund's business and financial plan may be decided by the Minister responsible for regional development up to the amount of the funds set out in the Fund's founding act. It is specified in more detail that the municipal council proposes a candidate for municipal representative to the development council of the region, and such a decision shall be adopted by the Council with a majority of the votes of all members; then the term of office of alternate member shall be from the adoption of the decision until the end of the term of office. This proposal simplifies the process of replacing members of the development council of the region and thus improves its functioning. It is therefore proposed to amend paragraph 5 by also excluding double funding, including for certain regions, such as the Law on the Development Support of the Pomur Region in the period 2010-2015, which could constitute a double eligibility for the extended implementation period of 2 years. That's what this law will do. It is also prevented that, due to Pmurje's special development law as a region with the greatest unemployment in the new financial perspective, certain measures of European cohesion policy and rural development policy would not be eligible for new resources. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u233	Mr Ivan Škodnik has the floor. Prepare Mr. Janko Weber and Primož Hainz.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u234	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Dear representatives of the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, colleagues and fellow Members! The amendments and additions to the Law on the Promotion of Coherent Regional Development are proposed in two areas. Firstly, the improvement of systemic solutions which, following the implementation of the Law on the Promotion of Coherent Regional Development in 2012, have proved to be weak or difficult to implement or, in breach of the amended European legislation on State aid and European cohesion policy and rural development policies, will facilitate the achievement of the objectives of the law. Secondly, rationalisation of administrative procedures that will facilitate the implementation of additional savings and use of public funds. We adopt the law under time pressure until the deadline set by the Law on Promoting coherent regional development of 2012, which is 28 July 2016. In five development agencies – Central Slovenian, Podravski, Pomurski, South-Eastern Slovenia and Coastal-Kraška – the world will not be able to reshape the ownership of the structure so that it meets the requirements of the law. As a result, some regional development agencies should be deleted from the register and this would have negative consequences for the development of these regions and for the overall policy of regional development. The proposal for a law therefore proposes abolishing the order that, within the framework of a public interest in a regional development agency, none of the municipalities should have more than a third of the founding share, so that, within a public institution, the share of the municipalities must be dispersed. In addition, the law solves the problem created by the adoption of the Law on the Development Support of the Pomur region in the period 2010-2015, which extended the implementation period of this law for 2 years, by eliminating the possibility of double eligibility of the Pomur development region to regional incentives. Other changes include: among instrument operators, programmes to promote competitiveness in problem areas with very high unemployment are added by institutions that already carry out the country's development tasks in the region. However, their inclusion is possible only if they are the same instruments, as it would not be rational for different institutions to implement them. In smaller transfers between items within the business and financial plan of the Republic of Slovenia’s Public Fund for Regional Development and Rural Development, the Government will no longer decide on them, but will be able to be decided by the competent minister up to the amount of funds determined by the founding act, which represents a more flexible regime. The lack of funding for the development of areas where the Roma community lives is addressed by a fund that has only recoverable resources that are not suitable for addressing the Roma problem, and it is therefore clear that these incentives are to be set in the national budget. A statutoryly defined ratio of funding for general development tasks at regional level of 60% of state resources and 40% of municipal resources is provided at annual level. The financing of projects under regional development arrangements shall be coordinated with current practice when funds can be provided by several ministers, ministries, taking into account eligibility in all development policies. The criterion for the designation of municipalities belonging to the problems mentioned above is supplemented by the inclusion of municipalities with a statutory development coefficient of less than 0,90 if they are bordering border municipalities or municipalities directly bordering border municipalities. The following are added: promotion and development of entrepreneurship and innovation culture, implementation of regional programmes and internationalisation of the economy, development of employment competences and spatial planning at regional level. For these reasons, I will support the bill. Thank you.

PERIOD OF THE DAY, THIS IS THE FIRST DEFINITIVE PROPOSAL OF A COLLECTIVE MANAGEMENT OF AUTHORITY AND COMMUNITY RIGHTS The draft law was tabled by the Government. With regard to this proposal, 18 Members and Members of Parliament, Mr Jozet Tank, first-signed, requested that the National Assembly hold a general debate. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, Mr Aleš Cantarutti.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u256	Dear President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Allow me a little more about the proposal for a law on collective management of copyright and related rights on the way to some clarifications. A proposal for a law before you that regulates the area of collective governance covered today by the Law on Copyright and Related Rights. Excluding the scope and placement in the new law of the European Union is followed by a Directive on collective management of copyright and related rights, which, at European level, aims to harmonise the participation of members in the adoption of key decisions of collective organisations, to regulate the management of collective organisations, to inform rightholders and foreign collective organisations, to establish transparency in the operations of collective organisations and other areas. A broad public debate was held on the bill. The first draft was forwarded to the public debate on 14 August 2015, the second draft in mid-December. During this time, four day-long substantive coordination meetings with authors, rightholders, collective organisations and users, as well as another interested public, were organised to exchange views on collective governance in Slovenia. The bill before you follows three goals. First of all, greater efficiency of the members of collective organisations in taking key decisions of collective organisations. Under two, greater transparency in the functioning of collective organisations and, last but not least, more effective control over the functioning of collective organisations and users of works. Greater involvement of the members of the collective organisation will be ensured through a clear management structure of the collective organisation composed of the Assembly, the Supervisory Board and the Management Board. Membership in a collective organisation will be based on objective, transparent and non-discriminatory rules. Members will be granted the right to participate in the Assembly and to take decisions on the core documents and issues of collective organisation, collection and distribution of copyright fees and fees and payments to dedicated funds. A minority of members will be allowed to convene the Assembly and appoint a special auditor to review the management of individual collective organisation business. The transparency of the activities of collective organisations shall be followed by provisions on the right of the right of the holder of the right to access documents and data of the collective organisation, including published annual reports and declarations of conflicts of interest and receipts of members of the supervisory committee and management. Collective organisations will be committed to thoroughly informing right-holders as well as foreign collective organisations, establishing effective mechanisms to resolve possible disputes. Decisions on the compulsory separate management of collected copyright fees, the use of collected copyright fees, deadlines for the allocation and payment of copyright fees and operating costs will also contribute to transparency of business. The draft law lays down measures that may be taken in the context of administrative supervision and fines in criminal proceedings. Proper supervision of the implementation of the provisions of the law is essential for the effective exercise of copyright and related rights in Slovenia. The Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Intellectual Property, as well as the Market Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia and the Agency for Public Records and Services, will play a central role in monitoring. The bill also contains other important provisions on the external operator, the use of works for non-commercial use, tariffs and common agreement, the copyright board, the possibility of issuing a common account and several territorial licences for the online use of musical works. We expect in the Government of the Republic of Slovenia that the implementation of the solutions presented will have positive effects on public perception of collective organisations and their role in Slovenia as well as the wider international space. The perception of people, in particular users, about the importance of respect for copyright and related rights could also change with greater respect for collective organisations. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u257	We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The text is given by the Group of the Modern Centre Party, Mrs Anita Kolesh.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u258	Dear Mr President, the ministerial team, dear colleagues and colleagues! We are looking at the arrangements for collective management of copyright and related rights that the applicant has removed from the Copyright and Related Rights Act and submitted to the National Assembly under a special law. This opens up the urgent process of recasting copyright and increases the transparency and regulation of the field of collective organisations, as has been successfully regulated by some countries with long experience in the field of copyright management. Collective governance generally needs updates or a framework for the operation of the collective organisation for the benefit of the authors and for the benefit of creativity. As early as 2005, the European Commission found that there was a need to improve the functioning of collective organisations, in the Commission's recommendations of 18 May 2005, in order for collective organisations to seek to represent the interests of rightholders, relations between rightholders and collective organisations should treat equally the different categories of rightholders and strive for fair and balanced representation of holders in the management of collective organisations. The rightholders should be informed by the collective organisation of the repertoire, of any reciprocal representation agreement, of the territory of the application of their powers and of the applicable tariffs. Similarly, collective organisations should fairly distribute all the collected compensations and specify the deductions they have made for purposes other than those for the management of the system. So in 2005 the Commission did. Dr. Miha Trumpus, an expert in copyright, says: "The importance of collective organisations comes directly from the importance of copyright as an instigator of creativity, innovation and cultural diversity. Since collective organisations take care of the implementation of this task in a large area of protected content, it is crucial for this task of copyright. This is especially true for small countries such as the Republic of Slovenia, where the effective functioning of collective organisations with all their functions is the protection of national and cultural identity." End of quote. Collective organisations are of great importance to rightholders; their collective, which gives them adequate bargaining power, provides them with regular marketing and recovery of fees and provides social and other support with solidarity. Collective organisations are the only way that authors can successfully stand up to powerful users and also against powerful right holders, most of them publishing giants. They are equally important for users who should explore with great problems and transaction costs who are the authors and right holders of work, the exercise of phonograms and other related rights they would like to use. Also in the light of this awareness, the legislator, taking into account the warnings and recommendations of the European Union, has formulated principles: the openness of the collective organisation to all rightholders, the efficiency of the management of collective organisations, the transparency of the business of collective organisations, the fair tariff for the use of copyright works, the efficiency of collecting and sharing of copyright holders and greater legal certainty. The principles are part of a proposal for a law that transposes Directive 2014/26/EU on collective management of copyright and related rights in Slovenia and the issuance of several territorial licences for rights of music works for online use in the internal market. The bill was the subject of a broad public debate, the first draft of the law was 14. 8. 2015 submitted to the public debate, which lasted until the end of September 2015. The second draft was held in public debate and intersectoral coordination from 14 December 2015. During this time, four day-long substantive coordination meetings with authors of rights holders, collective organisations and users and another public were organised to exchange views on collective governance in Slovenia. The law therefore pursues three key objectives: greater involvement of members of the collective organisation in taking key decisions of collective organisations, greater transparency in the functioning of collective organisations and more effective supervision of the functioning of collective organisations and users of works of copyright. Greater involvement will be ensured through clearly defined tasks of the bodies of the collective organisation – the Assembly, the Supervisory Committee, the management of which are the basic building blocks of the collective organisation, with which the effective management of copyright and related rights must be ensured. Membership in a collective organisation must be based on objective rules in order to ensure that all members have the right to participate in the assembly and thus have an impact on the functioning of the collective organisation. The responsibility of the Assembly shall be, inter alia, to adopt an annual report, to approve the contract with the external contractor, its amendments or cancellations. The members of the Management Board and the Supervisory Board will be jointly responsible for the damage resulting from the breach of their tasks. With regard to the division of copyright fees, the law strongly sets out the collective organisation for careful collection, management and division, while also laying down very clear rules on the creation of separate dedicated funds, which are of paramount importance for the promotion of cultural and artistic creation and the dissemination of new works of copyright. This part of the law especially follows the wishes or demands of the authors that we heard when we adopted the new Media Law. The new law will also specify the composition, procedures and decision-making of the copyright board. The Council is a relatively new institute in Slovenian copyright, which needs to be given the opportunity to justify its operation. The changes in the world are aimed at optimizing the decision-making process. The purpose of the proposed amendments is to enable the world to operate in full order, to make timely decisions that are justified and acceptable to all parties, thereby strengthening its trust and authority in the area of tariffs for the use of works of copyright. The transparency and transparency of the activities of collective organisations follow the provisions on the right of the right-holder to have access to documents and data of the collective organisation, including in the published annual reports of the collective organisation and the statement of conflicts of interest and disclosure of the remuneration of the members of the supervisory committee and management. Collective organisations will be committed to thoroughly informing right-holders and to establishing effective dispute resolution mechanisms. The provisions on the mandatory separate management of the collected copyright fees, the use of the collected copyright fees, the time limits for the allocation and payment of the collected copyright fees and the cost of the business will also contribute to the transparency of the business. The draft law systematically lays down the procedure and measures of the supervisory authority, which is the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Intellectual Property, while clearly defining its powers and at the same time the duties of the collective organisation with regard to the exercise of control. In accordance with the objective of effective supervision, the actions of the supervisory authority followed in the following order are also clearly defined: from the Regulation for the Elimination of Certain Violations to the possible recall of members of the management to the extreme measure – withdrawal of authorisation. At the same time as administrative supervision, the Apes Intellectual Property Office and the Market Inspectorate may, in the context of the supervision of the individual provisions of the law, impose additional fines. An important chapter of this law is also the settlement of disputes where the applicant retains the particular importance of an agreement on mediation, which is one of the most important institutions in the European Union for the resolution of disputes in the field of intellectual property or copyright. Pursuant to Article 167 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, the Union must take into account cultural diversity and contribute to the culture of the Member States, taking into account their national and regional development while at the same time putting the common cultural heritage at the forefront of its actions. Collective governance organisations have the right and must play an important role in the future in promoting the diversity of cultural expression by providing social, cultural and educational services for the benefit of rightholders, i.e. authors and the public. Since the law is well and thoughtfully prepared because it represents an appropriate implementation of the European Directive, because we have a serious intention to regulate the current issue of collective governance, to which we have committed ourselves also in the programme of the Modern Centre Party and the Coalition Treaty, and because we want to follow the tradition of regulating the area of collective organisations with all the characteristics of our system, we in the SMC Group have decided that we will support the proposal for a collective management of copyright and related rights in the first reading.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u275	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marusha Škopac, you have the floor. Get Mr. Tomaž Lisec ready.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-18-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u276	Thank you for your word, chairman. Good evening to all colleagues or everyone from the Ministry of Economic Development! The special law that we are discussing today is the proposal for a law on collective management of copyright and related rights, which is a suitable way to solve the problems of the existing collective governance system in the Republic of Slovenia not only by its content but also by its form, it is like a special law that has excluded the entire area of collective governance from the System Law on Copyright and Related Rights. I also believe that the purpose of the bill is not only to implement the European Collective Organisations Directive, but it is one of the many attempts to regulate the many challenges faced by the users of the collective management system in Slovenia. I'll explain why I think so. The proposal for a law on collective management of copyright and related rights is, in my view, a mere technical regulation that sets uniform minimum standards and conditions that the collective organisation must respect in its internal organisation and in the management of its monopolistic activity. To begin with, given that the areas in which collective organisations operate are mostly linked to culture, it is extremely important in today's debate on the collective management system not to mix the demands of professional, responsible, efficient and transparent collective organisations with problems related to the respect and exercise of copyright in Slovenia in general, with the question of the economic and social situation of creators in Slovenia and their existence, with questions about the global injustice of the intellectual property system and the monopolistic positions of global multinationals in the entertainment industry. The above-mentioned very important issues must be addressed, but not through other channels, but through legislation that regulates the working conditions of collective organisations. The bill that we are discussing today is therefore merely a technique that sets out how collective organisations in Slovenia should be organised and how they should be managed with the aim of being able to effectively collect as many copyrights as possible and without delay and with small costs to actually distribute to the final beneficiary, and how collective organisations should negotiate and conclude appropriate agreements with users and effectively resolve disputes with users. All other arguments, which see in the bill the destruction of Slovenian creators and Slovenian language and culture, are merely to cover up the real problems in collective governance and populism manipulation in the interest of individual interest groups. Otherwise, the very important conditions for cultural creation are the subject of the country's cultural policies and by no means of copyright. Let me remind you, collective organisations are a special feature of copyright that allows authors and right-holders to manage them more easily and efficiently in situations of mass exploitation of their works and allows users easy access to the legitimate use of many works. Thus, collective organisations are one of the basic infrastructures in the field of copyright that bring both to the creators, that is to the authors, as well as to the users of certain advantages and eliminate the problems and transaction costs arising from direct cooperation between creators and users. That is why I would like to emphasise today that collective organisations are very important and their existence is crucial for authors and users. Their effective and transparent operation strengthens confidence in the copyright system itself. Throughout history, the sole purpose of copyright was to encourage the creators of creation economically and morally. In fact, if the creator acquires the exclusive right of use on his own terms, this is the greatest incentive and motivation for him to create and survive. In view of the role of collective organisations, the credibility of authors and rightholders is crucial for their existence. There is no such confidence in Slovenia. If that is not the case, collective organisations do not carry out their mission and do not contribute to the perception of copyright as economic incentives for creation. Trust in the collective organisations in Slovenia is not for different more or less justified reasons. Among other things, because of the lack of knowledge and lack of respect for copyright such as such, domestic, discriminatory and sometimes ineffective and, in particular, imprudent activities of collective organisations, where, as a rule, the results of operations other than those of collective organisations are not clear to anyone. Not to the auditors themselves who confirm their financial statements many times. I also think that there is no confidence on the part of authors and users in the ability of the State to ensure the efficient and transparent functioning of collective organisations through appropriate regulatory measures and administrative measures. Therefore, it is our task, my fellow Members and colleagues, to confirm the proposed law and to safeguard copyright and its mission and to restore the confidence of all stakeholders in the collective governance system and thus in copyright by setting unified standards of business, operation and management of collective organisations. This will show that copyright, for some, the only source of survival, is completely equivalent to other forms of private property. The bill introduces new, higher standards of business of collective organisations that are fully in line with good practices in Europe, where there is an increasing professionalisation of the management of collective organisations by well-trained individuals. Collective organisations in Europe are losing their original union spirit. It seems to me that the most effective provisions allow for a single position that gives members of collective organisations the necessary levers – the so-called minority rights – and the right to documentation of a collective organisation, which can provide effective internal control over the work of collective organisations and their leadership and non-discrimination of individual categories of authors. I also strongly support a more transparent arrangement of promotion funds or dedicated funds for the purpose of promoting cultural and artistic creation and for social and educational purposes. Let me finish. I think that when the law is effectively implemented, it will allow greater confidence in the functioning of collective organisations and their leadership and thus restore confidence in the Institute of Copyright itself. With greater legitimacy and confidence from all stakeholders, collective organisations will also be easier to capitalise on all the good they have built in the years of their existence. However, I would like to see the Government of the Republic of Slovenia take a step further, and this year it will place even more important in the National Assembly than in the area of collective governance, also a thoroughly reformed Systemic Law on Copyright and Related Rights, which will adequately address the challenges of the information society and allow further restrictions on copyright in the undisputed public interest. Thank you.

I would also mention the implementation or control provided for in this proposal. Under the Directive, the State must establish procedures by which it can monitor the compliance of collective organisations with the provisions of the law. The bill follows the guidelines presented, provides for measures to be taken under administrative control, including fines in the criminal plan. If I list these administrative measures, they will be followed in the next order, following the principle of proportionality and gradual escalation of the legal coercion. First, the order, as it says, found violations. In the event of failure to remedy infringements, the competent authority shall, in the light of the gravity of the infringement itself, order the collective organisation to recall members of the management. It will follow, under three, the withdrawal of the permit as a last resort.
